# Wiper Arm/Nozzle Adjustment (5-door except Type-R) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021): Adjustment

Windshield

- Windshield Washer Nozzle - Check 1. Open the hood. 2. Check the shape of the windshield washer nozzle, and confirm whether it is type A or type B. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

1. Open the hood.

2. Check the shape of the windshield washer nozzle, and confirm whether it is type A or type B.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- Wiper Arm/Nozzle - Adjust (Type A) NOTICE: Adjust the washer nozzles by gently gripping, and then moving, the outside of each nozzle (A). Do not insert tools into the nozzle hole (B), as it may cause the washer fluid to spray incorrectly. Wiper Arms Auto-Stop Position 1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then turn the wiper switch ON, and then back OFF. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. When the wiper arms stop at the auto-stop position, make sure that they are at the correct position. a: Position at about 40.3 mm (1.587 in) from the end of the windshield to the end cap of the wiper blade (C). b: Position at about 38.5 mm (1.516 in) from the cowl cover (D) to the end cap of the wiper blade. Washer Nozzle Position 3. When you turn on the washer, make sure 50 % or more of the washer fluid lands within the spray area. If the spray area is not within the standard positions, adjust the nozzle(s). d: Position at about 250.3 mm (9.854 in) from the windshield center line (E). e: Position at about 142.6 mm (5.614 in) from the end of black ceramic gradation area (F) at the lower windshield.

NOTICE:

- Adjust the washer nozzles by gently gripping, and then moving, the outside of each nozzle (A).

- Do not insert tools into the nozzle hole (B), as it may cause the washer fluid to spray incorrectly.

Wiper Arms Auto-Stop Position

1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode, then turn the wiper switch ON, and then back OFF.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

2. When the wiper arms stop at the auto-stop position, make sure that they are at the correct position.

a: Position at about 40.3 mm (1.587 in) from the end of the windshield to the end cap of the wiper blade (C).

b: Position at about 38.5 mm (1.516 in) from the cowl cover (D) to the end cap of the wiper blade.

Washer Nozzle Position

3. When you turn on the washer, make sure 50 % or more of the washer fluid lands within the spray area. If the spray area is not within the standard positions, adjust the nozzle(s).

d: Position at about 250.3 mm (9.854 in) from the windshield center line (E).

e: Position at about 142.6 mm (5.614 in) from the end of black ceramic gradation area (F) at the lower windshield.

- Wiper Arm/Nozzle - Adjust (Type B) NOTICE: Adjust the washer nozzles by gently gripping, and then moving, the outside of each nozzle (A). Do not insert tools into the nozzle hole (B), as it may cause the washer fluid to spray incorrectly. Wiper Arms Auto-Stop Position 1. ## Chunk 3248: Automatic Lighting/Rain Sensor Input Test

- Title: Automatic Lighting/Rain Sensor Input Test
- Source path: `pages\2339.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_f21804a7359a`
- Images: `images\GHH407557.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3022.html`, `pages\25526.html`, `pages\14277.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Automatic Lighting/Rain Sensor Input Test

NOTE:

- If you are troubleshooting multiple DTCs, be sure to follow the instructions in B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

- Before testing, make sure the No. A18 (10 A) fuse in the under-hood fuse/relay box is OK.

1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

2. Disconnect the automatic lighting/rain sensor 5P connector .

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

3. Inspect the connector and socket terminals to be sure they are all making good contact:

- If the terminals are bent, loose, or corroded, repair them as necessary, and recheck the system.

- If the terminals are OK, go to step 4.

4. Reconnect the connector, and do the following input tests:

- If any test indicates a problem, find and correct the cause, then recheck the system.

- If all the input tests prove OK, replace the automatic lighting/rain sensor .

Cavity | Wire | Test condition | Test: Desired result | Possible cause if desired result is not obtained

1 | YEL | Combination light switch OFF | Measure the voltage to ground: There should be battery voltage. | An open or high resistance in the wire

2 | BLK | In all power modes | Measure the voltage to ground: There should be less than 0.2 V. | Poor ground (G502) An open or high resistance in the ground wire

- Poor ground (G502)

- An open or high resistance in the ground wire

5 | RED | Under all conditions | Measure the voltage to ground: There should be battery voltage. | Blown No. A18 (10 A) fuse in the under-hood fuse/relay box An open or high resistance in the wire

- Blown No. A18 (10 A) fuse in the under-hood fuse/relay box

- An open or high resistance in the wire
````

## Chunk 3249: Wipers/Washers (Body Control Module and Windshield Wiper Control Unit) Input Test (2/4-door)

- Title: Wipers/Washers (Body Control Module and Windshield Wiper Control Unit) Input Test (2/4-door)
- Source path: `pages\2340.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_9c01d163d3d2`
- Images: `images\GHH407558.jpeg`, `images\GHH407559.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3023.html`, `pages\25527.html`, `pages\14278.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Wipers/Washers (Body Control Module and Windshield Wiper Control Unit) Input Test (2/4-door)

NOTE:

- If you are troubleshooting multiple DTCs, be sure to follow the instructions in B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

- Before testing, make sure the No. A2-5 and No. A6 fuses are OK.

- Before testing, make sure the No. B8 and No. B11* fuses are OK. *: Except L15BY engine

*: Except L15BY engine

Body Control Module

1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

2. Disconnect body control module connectors A, B, C, D, and E .

NOTE: All connector views are shown from the wire side of the female terminals.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

3. Inspect the connectors and socket terminals to be sure they are all making good contact:

- If the terminals are bent, loose, or corroded, repair them as necessary, and recheck the system.

- If the terminals are OK, go to step 4.

4. With the connectors still disconnected, do the following input tests:

- If any test indicates a problem, find and correct the cause, then recheck the system.

- If all the input tests prove OK, go to step 5.

# Wipers/Washers (Body Control Module and Windshield Wiper Control Unit) Input Test (5-door) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

NOTE:

- If you are troubleshooting multiple DTCs, be sure to follow the instructions in B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

- Before testing, make sure the No. A6 (15 A) and No. A2-5 (30 A) fuses in the under-hood fuse/relay box are OK.

- Before testing, make sure the No. B3 (10 A), No. B7 (10 A) *1, No. B8 (15 A) *3/(20 A) *4, and No. B21 (10 A) *2 fuses in the under-dash fuse/relay box is OK.

*1: CVT

*2: M/T

*3: Except K20C1 engine

*4: K20C1 engine

Body Control Module

1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

2. Disconnect body control module connectors A, B, C, D, and E.

NOTE: All connector views are shown from the wire side of the female terminals.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

3. Inspect the connectors and socket terminals to be sure they are all making good contact:

- If the terminals are bent, loose, or corroded, repair them as necessary, and recheck the system.

- If the terminals are OK, go to step 4.

4. With the connectors still disconnected, do the following input tests:

- If any test indicates a problem, find and correct the cause, then recheck the system.

- If all the input tests prove OK, go to step 5.

- Faulty rear window wiper motor

- Poor ground (G603) or an open in the ground wire

- An open or high resistance in the wire

B6 | GRY | Under all conditions | Connect to ground with a jumper wire: The windshield/rear window washer motor should run. | Blown No. A6 (15 A) fuse in the under-hood fuse/relay box Faulty rear window washer motor relay Faulty windshield/rear window washer motor An open or high resistance in the wire

- Blown No. A6 (15 A) fuse in the under-hood fuse/relay box

- Faulty rear window washer motor relay

- Faulty windshield/rear window washer motor

- An open or high resistance in the wire

B12 | BRN | Under all conditions | Connect to ground with a jumper wire: The windshield/rear window washer motor should run. | Blown No. A6 (15 A) fuse in the under-hood fuse/relay box Faulty windshield washer motor relay circuit Faulty windshield/rear window washer motor An open or high resistance in the wire

- Blown No. A6 (15 A) fuse in the under-hood fuse/relay box

- Faulty windshield washer motor relay circuit

- Faulty windshield/rear window washer motor

- An open or high resistance in the wire

C13 | YEL | Disconnect the windshield wiper control unit/motor 6P connector | Check for continuity between terminal C13 and windshield wiper control unit/motor 6P connector terminal No. 4: There should be continuity. | An open or high resistance in the wire

5. Reconnect the connectors to the body control module, and do the following input tests:

- If any test indicates a problem, find and correct the cause, then recheck the system.

- If all the input tests prove OK, replace the body control module .

Cavity | Wire | Test condition | Test: Desired result | Possible cause if desired result is not obtained

E1 | BLU | Vehicle ON mode | Measure the voltage to ground: There should be battery voltage. | Blown No. B8 (15 A) *4/(20 A) *5 fuse in the under-dash fuse/relay box An open or high resistance in the wire

- Blown No. B8 (15 A) *4/(20 A) *5 fuse in the under-dash fuse/relay box

- An open or high resistance in the wire

A2 | BLK | In all power modes | Measure the voltage to ground: There should be less than 0.2 V. | Poor ground (G501) An open or high resistance in the ground wire

- An open or high resistance in the ground wire

A6 | BLK | In all power modes | Measure the voltage to ground: There should be less than 0.2 V. | Poor ground (G501) An open or high resistance in the ground wire

- Poor ground (G501)

## Chunk 3261: Wipers/Washers System Description - Components

- Title: Wipers/Washers System Description - Components
- Source path: `pages\2347.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_c8aa9cc3c09b`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3030.html`, `pages\25534.html`, `pages\14285.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Wipers/Washers System Description - Components

Wiper/Washer Switch

The wiper/washer switch inputs the switch status to the body control module. A backup of the windshield wiper control unit/motor is performed via the body control module the during LO or HI switch operation in case an abnormality of the wiper/washer switch or the input of the LO and HI line is detected by the LIN.

Automatic Lighting/Rain Sensor (With Automatic Wiper)

The auto wiper signal or the auto wiper fail signal is sent via the B-CAN to the body control module.

Rear Window Wiper Motor (With Rear Window Wiper)

The rear window wiper motor is controlled by the body control module. When the motor temperature becomes high because of an overload etc., the motor is stopped temporarily by its circuit breaker, interrupting the circuit.
````

## Chunk 3262: Wipers/Washers System Description - Control/Function

- Title: Wipers/Washers System Description - Control/Function
- Source path: `pages\2348.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_eb3e2105102e`
- Images: `images\GHH407572.jpeg`, `images\GHH407573.jpeg`, `images\GHH407574.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3031.html`, `pages\25535.html`, `pages\14286.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Wipers/Washers System Description - Control/Function

Basic Control/Function

The intelligent controlled wiper system detects the operating angle of the windshield wiper motor in order to recognize the wiping position of the wiper blade assembly. The wiping speed of the windshield wiper motor is controlled according to a target speed map corresponding to the wiping speed (Low or High) to perform wiping speed control. This wiping speed control keeps the wiping speed of the wiper blade assembly at a constant.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Parking Position Operation

During wiper operation, the parking position operation is performed when any of the following conditions are met:

- When the vehicle is turned to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

- When the wiper/washer switch is turned off.

- When the wiper/washer switch is in AUTO position, but the automatic wiper signal is not sent from the automatic lighting/rain sensor.

Break-Away Operation

The intelligent controlled wiper system performs blockage detection during wiper operation and performs obstacle clearance control upon detection. The operation of the obstacle clearance control is in the following: If the wiper motor is detected to have stopped for 200 ms or more continuously while operating at maximum drive, a blockage is detected. Reverse action is used if a blockage is detected outside the reversal limit range. If a blockage is detected inside the reversal limit range, the wiper motor stops operation for 800 ms, then resumes wiping. (Blockage removal action) After blockage removal action is detected (counted) five times within the reversal limit range, the wiper is judged to be in a locked state, and the wiper motor stops. After the wiper motor stops, it restarts when the wiper/washer switch is turned off and on again.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Service Position

Operating Condition

When the wiper is active or stops at any position lower than the upper reversal position, turn and hold the MIST switch, and then turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode to stop the wiper blades at the service position.

End Conditions

- Vehicle is in the ON mode.

- The upper reversing position is recognized.

Rear Window Wiper Control

The body control module performs control according to the wiper switch signal, the back-up light signal, the ATP-R signal, the washer switch signal, and other input data. Wiper and washer linkage or reverse linkage is set.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Automatic Lighting/Rain Sensor Connection Check (With Automatic Wiper)

The body control module spends about a second to check if automatic lighting/rain sensor is connected every time a battery is connected to the body control module. When the body control module does not detect the connection within the time period, the automatic wiper function does not work.
````

## Chunk 3263: Wipers/Washers System Description - Failsafe Function

- Title: Wipers/Washers System Description - Failsafe Function
- Source path: `pages\2349.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_dc17c9f5a1ea`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3032.html`, `pages\25536.html`, `pages\14287.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Wipers/Washers System Description - Failsafe Function

Hardware Failsafe

The control module of the CPU for the windshield wiper control unit/motor in case of an abnormality is as shown in the table.

Output Name | Fixed Value

Motor Drive Circuit | All OFF

LIN Communication Circuit | OFF

Body Control Module Frame Error

In case of a bus off error or wiper/washer switch signal error, the failsafe operation is as follows:

NOTE: If the back up line switches from OFF to ON, failsafe operation starts with Step 1.

Back Up Line | Failsafe Operation When Wiper is Active | Failsafe operation When Wiper is Not Active

1 | OFF | Parking operation (Except for wiping in AUTO mode) | No operation (Remains stopped)

2 | ON | Step 1 | HIGH operation for 3 minutes -> Step 2

Step 2 | LOW operation for 3 minutes -> Step 3

Step 3 | Intermittent operation (speed-dependent variable mode) for 12 minutes -> Step 4

Step 4 | Parking operation

Body Control Module Frame Error in Auto Mode Operation

If a wiper switch signal error occurs while the wipers are operating in auto mode, the failsafe operation is as follows:

Previous Operation in AUTO Mode | Failsafe Operation

1 | HIGH | Step 1 | HIGH operation for 3 minutes -> Step 2

2 | LOW | Step 2 | LOW operation for 3 minutes -> Step 3

3 | INT, Lower reversal position, or Parking position | Step 3 | Intermittent operation (speed-dependent variable mode) for 12 minutes -> Step 4

Step 4 | Parking operation

BACK UP LINE and LIN Inconsistency Error

If an error occurs due to inconsistency between the back up line and LIN (IG1 signal and wiper switch signal), the failsafe operation is as follows:

Back Up Line | IG1 | Wiper Switch Position | Failsafe Operation

ON | ON | LOW and HIGH Switch OFF | HIGH

OFF | -- | Step 1 | HIGH for 3 minutes -> Step 2

Step 2 | LOW for 3 minutes -> Step 3

Step 3 | Intermittent operation (speed-dependent variable mode) for 12 minutes -> Step 4

Step 4 | Parking operation

Automatic Wiper Frame Error

If the automatic lighting/rain sensor is faulty or an abnormal automatic wiper signal is detected, the failsafe operation is as follows:

Previous Operation in AUTO Mode | Failsafe Operation

1 | HIGH | Step 1 | HIGH for 3 minutes -> Step 2

2 | LOW | Step 2 | LOW for 3 minutes -> Step 3

3 | INT | Step 3 | Intermittent operation (speed-dependent variable mode)

4 | Lower reversal position or Parking position

Thermal Protection Function

The intelligent controlled wiper system performs thermal protection control to prevent excess heat generation by the windshield wiper control unit/motor due to abnormal load. If the temperature of the windshield wiper control unit/motor exceeds the protective action temperature, the wiping speed is reduced, and is restored once the temperature falls below the reset action temperature.
````

# Removal and Installation

SRS components are located in this area. Review the SRS component locations and the precautions and procedures before doing repairs or service.

- 12 Volt Battery Terminal - Disconnect NOTE: Wait at least 3 minutes before starting work.

NOTE: Wait at least 3 minutes before starting work.

- Both Front Seats - Remove

- Center Console - Remove

- Both A/B/Quarter Pillar Trims - Remove

- Both Sunvisors/Sunvisor Holders - Remove

- All Grab Handles - Remove

- Ceiling Light - Remove

- Front Individual Map Light - Remove

- Connector (Multipurpose Camera Unit) - Disconnect (For Some Models)

- Connector (Rearview Mirror) - Disconnect (With Automatic Dimming Mirror)

- Connector (Automatic Lighting/Rain Sensor) - Disconnect (With Automatic Wiper)

- High Mount Brake Light Cover - Remove

- Roof Console - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Remove the roof console (A). 2. With HFL microphone: Disconnect the connector (B).

2. With HFL microphone: Disconnect the connector (B).

For some models: Remove the harness clip (A) at the left quarter pillar. 6. Disconnect the connector (B) at the right quarter pillar. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 7. With the help of an assistant, lower the headliner (A). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 8. With the help of an assistant, remove the headliner (A) through the door opening. # Removal and Installation

NOTE: SRS components are located in this area. Review the SRS component locations and the precautions and procedures before doing repairs or service.

A-Pillar Trim

- A-Pillar Trim - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

If the side curtain airbag has not deployed, check the A-pillar trim and note the following items: To prevent the side curtain airbags from deploying improperly and possibly causing injury, inspect the A-pillar trim and replace it with a new one if it has any of the following damage: Any cracks, deformations, or stress-whitened areas in the A-pillar trim Any cracks or stress-whitening in the clip seating surface (B) If the clip (C) is damaged or stress-whitened, replace it with a new one.

NOTE:

- If side airbags and/or side curtain airbags have deployed, the affected pillar area trim and attachment clips must be replaced. Refer to Component Replacement/Inspection After Deployment .

- C-Pillar Lower Trim - Remove

- Rear Shelf Trim - Remove 1. Fold down the rear seat-back. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. From the trunk lid opening, disconnect the connector (A). 3. With rear tweeter: From the trunk lid opening, disconnect the connectors (B). 4. Remove the rear shelf trim (C).

2. From the trunk lid opening, disconnect the connector (A).

3. With rear tweeter: From the trunk lid opening, disconnect the connectors (B).

4. Remove the rear shelf trim (C).

- All Removed Parts - Install 1. Install the parts in the reverse order of removal.

1. Install the parts in the reverse order of removal.

NOTE: If the sunvisor cannot be removed, the hook has not rotated into the bracket cover. Repeat step 2 to rotate the hook. 4. With vanity mirror light: Disconnect the connector (B).

# Sunvisor Removal and Installation: Installation

- Sunvisor - Check Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. If the side curtain airbag has deployed, replace the sunvisor, along with the other parts listed for side curtain airbag deployment. 2. If the side curtain airbag has not deployed, inspect the sunvisor for damage. A damaged sunvisor may cause the side curtain airbags to deploy improperly, possibly causing injury. Replace the sunvisor if it has any of the following damage: Any cracks in the sunvisor bracket (A) Any cracks in the sunvisor bracket cover (B) Any bends or cracks in the sunvisor shaft (C) Any cracks in the sunvisor base (D) Any cracks in the vanity mirror base (E) 3. If the holder grommet is damaged or stress-whitened, replace the holder grommet and the holder as an assembly.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. If the side curtain airbag has deployed, replace the sunvisor, along with the other parts listed for side curtain airbag deployment. 2. If the side curtain airbag has not deployed, inspect the sunvisor for damage. A damaged sunvisor may cause the side curtain airbags to deploy improperly, possibly causing injury. Replace the sunvisor if it has any of the following damage: Any cracks in the sunvisor bracket (A) Any cracks in the sunvisor bracket cover (B) Any bends or cracks in the sunvisor shaft (C) Any cracks in the sunvisor base (D) Any cracks in the vanity mirror base (E) 3. If the holder grommet is damaged or stress-whitened, replace the holder grommet and the holder as an assembly.

2. If the side curtain airbag has not deployed, inspect the sunvisor for damage. A damaged sunvisor may cause the side curtain airbags to deploy improperly, possibly causing injury. Replace the sunvisor if it has any of the following damage:

- Any cracks in the sunvisor bracket (A)

- Any cracks in the sunvisor bracket cover (B)

- Any bends or cracks in the sunvisor shaft (C)

- Any cracks in the sunvisor base (D)

- Any cracks in the vanity mirror base (E)

3. If the holder grommet is damaged or stress-whitened, replace the holder grommet and the holder as an assembly.

- Sunvisor Holder - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Install the holder (A) into the holder grommet (B) by turning it 45° clockwise. 2. Install the holder and holder grommet as an assembly into the body by pushing it until the hooks snap into place securely.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Install the holder (A) into the holder grommet (B) by turning it 45° clockwise. 2. Install the holder and holder grommet as an assembly into the body by pushing it until the hooks snap into place securely.

2. Install the holder and holder grommet as an assembly into the body by pushing it until the hooks snap into place securely.

- Sunvisor - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. With vanity mirror light: Connect the vanity mirror light connector (A). 2. Install the sunvisor (B). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. While holding the bracket cover (A), rotate the sunvisor (B) forward until the hook (C) snaps into place. 4. Gently pull down on the sunvisor to make sure it is properly secured in the body. 5. Rotate the sunvisor into the holder (D).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. With vanity mirror light: Connect the vanity mirror light connector (A). 2. Install the sunvisor (B).

4. Gently pull down on the sunvisor to make sure it is properly secured in the body.

5. Rotate the sunvisor into the holder (D).

# Entry Lights Control System (Body Control Module and Door Multiplex Control Unit) Input Test (2/4-door)

NOTE:

- If you are troubleshooting multiple DTCs, be sure to follow the instructions in B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

- Before testing, make sure the No. A18 and No. A21 fuses are OK.

- Before testing, make sure the No. B8 and No. B11* fuses are OK. *: Except L15BY engine

*: Except L15BY engine

Body Control Module

1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

2. Disconnect body control module connectors A, C, D, and E .

NOTE: All connector views are shown from the wire side of the female terminals.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Inspect the connectors and socket terminals to be sure they are all making good contact:

- If the terminals are bent, loose, or corroded, repair them as necessary, and recheck the system.

- If the terminals look OK, go to step 4.

Reconnect the connectors to the body control module, and do the following input tests:

- If any test indicates a problem, find and correct the cause, then recheck the system.

- If all the input tests prove OK, then go to step 5.

- Faulty front passenger's door switch ground

# Entry Lights Control System (Body Control Module and Door Multiplex Control Unit) Input Test (5-door) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

NOTE:

- If you are troubleshooting multiple DTCs, be sure to follow the instructions in B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

*2: K20C1 engine

Body Control Module

1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

2. Disconnect body control module connectors A, C, D, and E.

NOTE: All connector views are shown from the wire side of the female terminals.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Inspect the connectors and socket terminals to be sure they are all making good contact:

- If the terminals are bent, loose, or corroded, repair them as necessary, and recheck the system.

- If the terminals look OK, go to step 4.

Reconnect the connectors to the body control module, and do the following input tests:

# Ignition Key Light Test: Test

SRS components are located in this area. Review the SRS component locations and the precautions and procedures before doing repairs or service.

- Column Covers - Remove

- Ignition Key Light - Test Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Disconnect the connector (A). 2. Check the light according to the table. NOTE: When an LED is located between terminals, check if the LED illuminates by connecting power and ground to the LED. Note this important operating characteristic; diode bias causes a diode to fully conduct electricity in one direction (forward), while not at all in the opposite direction (reverse). 3. If the result is not as specified, the ignition key light is faulty; replace the steering lock (B).

## Chunk 3356: Interior Lights System Description - Components

- Title: Interior Lights System Description - Components
- Source path: `pages\2434.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_6a3ed54af036`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\18620.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Interior Lights System Description - Components

Individual Map Lights

The individual map lights are equipped on all vehicles. The individual map lights can be turned on and off by pressing on the map light lens. A mechanical switch illuminates the individual map light regardless of conditions.

Ambient Light

The ambient light is placed between the front individual map lights. The ambient lights illuminate when the taillight relay is ON, and can be adjusted by the gauge control module.

Vanity Mirror Lights

The vanity mirror lights are placed on both sunvisors, and illuminates when the vanity mirror cover opens.

Ceiling Light

The ceiling light is placed in the center of the headliner. The ceiling light illuminates when the ceiling light switch is ON.

Upper Tray Light and Lower Tray Light (With Tray Lights)

The upper tray light and lower tray light are placed in the front of the center console, illuminates when the taillight relay is ON, and can be adjusted by the gauge control module.

Trunk Light (2/4-door)

The trunk light is placed in the trunk cargo area, and illuminates when the trunk lid opens.

Cargo Area Light (5-door)

The cargo area light is placed in the right rear side trim, and illuminates when the tailgate opens.

Glove Box Light (2/4-door)

The glove box light illuminates when the taillight relay is ON.

Glove Box Light (5-door)

The glove box light is placed inside the glove box. The glove box light illuminates when the glove box opens.
````

## Chunk 3357: Interior Lights System Description - Control/Function

- Title: Interior Lights System Description - Control/Function
- Source path: `pages\2435.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_ad4b9eba1e69`
- Images: `images\GHH409442.jpeg`, `images\GHH409443.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\18621.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Interior Lights System Description - Control/Function

System Function

Initial state of connection of electric power supply

In the initial state of connection of electric power supply, close/open of each door is detected and controlled without being affected by states of the vehicle mode and the driver's door lock knob switch.

Input | Output Control

Each Door Switch | Vehicle Mode | Before Input Change | After Input Change

Any ON(Door open) | ACCESSORY or ON | -- | Lights ON

OFF (LOCK) | -- | Lights ON by timer condition 3

All OFF(Door close) | -- | -- | Lights OFF

NOTE: The "--" means that it is not used on this system.

Driver's door lock knob switch link

When the driver's door lock knob switch moves from the lock/unlock position, the body control module detects a driver's door open/close position, and controls the interior lights.

Input | Output Control

Driver's Door Lock Knob Switch (ON: LOCK) | Each Door Switch | Vehicle Mode | Before Input Change | After Input Change

ON -> OFF | All OFF(Door close) | Remote Engine Start | Lighting ON | Lights ON by timer condition 1

Lighting OFF | Lights ON by timer condition 5

OFF (LOCK) | Lighting ON | Lights ON by timer condition 1

Lighting OFF | Lights ON by timer condition 5

ACCESSORY | -- | Continue the status before input change

Any ON(Door open) | OFF (LOCK) or ACCESSORY | --

OFF-> ON | All OFF(Door close) | Remote Engine Start | -- | Lights OFF

OFF (LOCK) or ACCESSORY | --

Any ON(Door open) | OFF (LOCK) or ACCESSORY | -- | Continue the statusbefore input change

NOTE: The "--" means that it is not used on this system.

Each door switch link

Anytime a door switch closes (ON signal), the body control module provides a ground to the interior lights.

Input | Output Control

Each Door Switch | Driver's Door Lock Knob Switch (ON: LOCK) | Vehicle Mode | Before Input Change | After Input Change

Any ON(Door open)->All OFF(Door close) | OFF | Remote Engine Start | Lighting ON | Lights ON by timer condition 2

Lighting OFF | Continue the status before input change

OFF (LOCK) | Lighting ON | Lights ON by timer condition 2

Lighting OFF | Continue the status before input change

ACCESSORY or ON | -- | Lights OFF

ON | -- | --

NOTE: The "--" means that it is not used on this system.

Input | Output Control

Each Door Switch | Driver's Door Lock Knob Switch (ON: LOCK) | Vehicle Mode | Before Input Change | After Input Change

Any OFF(Door close)->ON (Door open) | -- | Remote Engine Start | Lighting ON | Lights ON by timer condition 3 (Timer restart)

Lighting OFF | Lights ON by timer condition 3

OFF (LOCK) | Lighting ON | Lights ON by timer condition 3 (Timer restart)

Lighting OFF | Lights ON by timer condition 3

ACCESSORY or ON | -- | Lights ON

NOTE: The "--" means that it is not used on this system.

Ignition key switch/Remote link

When the system detects that the key is inserted into the ignition key switch or the remote is inside the vehicle, it provides a ground to the interior lights.

Input | Output Control

Vehicle Mode | Each Door Switch | Before Input Change | After Input Change

ACCESSORY -> OFF (LOCK) | All Door Switch OFF (Close) | Lighting ON | Lights ON by timer condition 1

Lighting OFF | Lights ON by timer condition 5

Any Door Switch ON (Open) | -- | Lights ON by timer condition 3

OFF (LOCK) -> ACCESSORY | All Door Switch OFF (Close) | -- | Continue the status before input change

Any Door Switch ON (Open) | -- | Lights ON

NOTE: The "--" means that it is not used on this system.

Engine start/stop switch link

If the vehicle mode turns to the ACCESSORY mode or the ON mode with the doors closed and the entry light control system illuminating the start/stop switch lights, the body control module fades the lights off.

Input | Output Control

Vehicle Mode | Each Door Switch | Before Input Change | After Input Change

Remote Engine Start | All OFF | -- | Lights OFF

Any ON | -- | Lights ON by timer condition 3

ON -> ACCESSORY | -- | -- | Continue the status before input change

ACCESSORY -> ON | All OFF | -- | Lights OFF

Any ON | -- | Lights ON

ON | All OFF | -- | Lights OFF

Any ON | -- | Lights ON

NOTE: The "--" means that it is not used on this system.

Keyless link

When the body control module receives an unlock signal from the keyless transmitter or remote, it provides a ground to the interior lights.

Input | Output Control
````

# Removal and Installation

- Mirror Holder - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Adjust the mirror fully to the upward position. 2. Put a shop towel in the opening between the bottom edge of the mirror holder (A) and the mirror housing (B) to prevent scratches. 3. Insert a flat-tip screwdriver under the mirror holder, and release the hooks (C). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 4. Remove the mirror holder (A). 5. Disconnect the connectors (B).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Adjust the mirror fully to the upward position. 2. Put a shop towel in the opening between the bottom edge of the mirror holder (A) and the mirror housing (B) to prevent scratches. 3. Insert a flat-tip screwdriver under the mirror holder, and release the hooks (C).

2. Put a shop towel in the opening between the bottom edge of the mirror holder (A) and the mirror housing (B) to prevent scratches.

3. Insert a flat-tip screwdriver under the mirror holder, and release the hooks (C).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 4. Remove the mirror holder (A). 5. Disconnect the connectors (B).

5. Disconnect the connectors (B).

- All Removed Parts - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Install the parts in the reverse order of removal. NOTE: Before reinstalling the mirror holder to the inner holder (A) of the actuator, make sure the actuator rods (B) are securely mounted in the holes, and that the boots (C) properly cover each rod.

## Chunk 3378: Automatic Dimming Inside Mirror Connector for Inputs and Outputs (2/4-door)

- Title: Automatic Dimming Inside Mirror Connector for Inputs and Outputs (2/4-door)
- Source path: `pages\2455.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_0ac30cc2efbb`
- Images: `images\GHH410446.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3108.html`, `pages\25425.html`, `pages\18583.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Automatic Dimming Inside Mirror Connector for Inputs and Outputs (2/4-door)

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Terminal number | Terminal name | Description | Signal

1 * | +B BACK UP | Power source for HomeLink unit | About battery voltage at all time

2 * | ILLUMI+ | Detects ILLUMI+ signal | With parking lights are ON: about battery voltage With parking lights are OFF: less than 0.2 V

3 | Not used | --- | ---

4 | Not used | --- | ---

5 | BACK LT | Detects BACK LT signal | With the vehicle in the ON mode and R position/mode: about battery voltage If none of the above conditions are met: less than 0.2 V

6 | GND | Ground for automatic dimming inside mirror | Less than 0.2 V at all times

7 | IG2 A/C | IG2 power source | With the vehicle in the ON mode: about battery voltage

*: With HomeLink
````

## Chunk 3380: Power Mirrors (Front Passenger's Power Window Switch) Input Test

- Title: Power Mirrors (Front Passenger's Power Window Switch) Input Test
- Source path: `pages\2457.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_7d4b5d3e334c`
- Images: `images\GHH410448.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3110.html`, `pages\25427.html`, `pages\18585.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Power Mirrors (Front Passenger's Power Window Switch) Input Test

NOTE:

- Before testing, check for DTCs. If any DTCs are indicated, troubleshoot those DTCs first.

- Before testing, make sure the No. B15 (20 A) fuse in the under-dash fuse/relay box is OK.

1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

2. Disconnect the front passenger's power window switch 37P connector .

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Inspect the connector and socket terminals to be sure they are all making good contact:

- If the terminals are bent, loose, or corroded, repair them as necessary, and recheck the system.

- If the terminals look OK, go to step 4.

With the connector still disconnected, do the following input tests:

- If any test indicates a problem, find and correct the cause, then recheck the system.

- If all the input tests prove OK, go to step 5.

Cavity | Wire | Test condition | Test: Desired result | Possible cause if desired result is not obtained

19*17 | BLU*YEL | Vehicle ON mode, connect terminals No. 1 and No. 17, and terminals No. 19 and No. 3 with jumper wires. | Check the right power mirror operation: The right power mirror should move LEFT. | Faulty right power mirror Poor ground (G505) or an open in the ground wire An open or high resistance in the wire

- Faulty right power mirror

- Poor ground (G505) or an open in the ground wire

- An open or high resistance in the wire

Vehicle ON mode, connect terminals No. 1 and No. 19, and terminals No. 17 and No. 3 with jumper wires. | Check the right power mirror operation: The right power mirror should move RIGHT.

17*18 | YEL*LT GRN | Connect terminals No. 1 and No. 18, and terminals No. 17 and No. 3 with jumper wires. | Check the right power mirror operation: The right power mirror should move UP. | Faulty right power mirror Poor ground (G505) or an open in the ground wire An open or high resistance in the wire

- Faulty right power mirror

- Poor ground (G505) or an open in the ground wire

- An open or high resistance in the wire

Connect terminals No. 1 and No. 17, and terminals No. 18 and No. 3 with jumper wires. | Check the right power mirror operation: The right power mirror should move DOWN.

*1: With mirror defogger

*2: With moonroof

Cavity | Wire | Test condition | Test: Desired result | Possible cause if desired result is not obtained

23 *1 | LT BLU | Vehicle ON mode, connect terminals No. 1 and No. 23 with a jumper wire | Check the right power mirror defogger operation: The right power mirror defogger should work (the inside lower edge becomes warm). | Faulty right power mirror defogger Poor ground (G505) or an open in the ground wire An open or high resistance in the wire

- Faulty right power mirror defogger

- Poor ground (G505) or an open in the ground wire

- An open or high resistance in the wire

34 | WHT | Disconnect these connectors: Power window master switch 37P connector Moonroof motor-control unit 14P connector *2 | Check for continuity between terminal No. 34 and power window master switch 37P connector terminal No. 36: There should be continuity. | An open in the LIN(P/W) wire

- Power window master switch 37P connector

- Moonroof motor-control unit 14P connector *2

Check for continuity between terminal No. 34 and moonroof motor-control unit 14P connector terminal No. 12: There should be continuity. | An open in the LIN(P/W) wire

Check for continuity to ground: There should be no continuity. | A short to ground in the LIN(P/W) wire

*1: With mirror defogger

*2: With moonroof

5. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. Reconnect the connector, and do the following input tests:

- If any test indicates a problem, find and correct the cause, then recheck the system.

- If all the input tests prove OK, replace the front passenger's power window switch .

NOTE: After replacing the front passenger's power window switch, do the resetting the power window control unit .

Cavity | Wire | Test condition | Test: Desired result | Possible cause if desired result is not obtained

1 | GRN | Under all conditions | Measure the voltage to ground: There should be battery voltage. | Blown No. B15 (20 A) fuse in the under-dash fuse/relay box An open or high resistance in the wire

- Blown No. B15 (20 A) fuse in the under-dash fuse/relay box

- An open or high resistance in the wire

3 | BLK | In all power modes | Measure the voltage to ground: There should be less than 0.2 V. | Poor ground (G505) An open or high resistance in the ground wire
````

NOTE:

- Before testing, check for DTCs. If any DTCs are indicated, troubleshoot those DTCs first.

- Before testing, make sure the No. B15 (20 A) fuse in the under-dash fuse/relay box is OK.

1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

2. Disconnect the front passenger's power window switch 37P connector.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Inspect the connector and socket terminals to be sure they are all making good contact:

## Chunk 3390: Automatic Dimming Mirrors Test (2/4-door)

# Automatic Dimming Mirrors Test (2/4-door)

1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. Measure the voltage between terminal No. 7 and body ground.

- If there is battery voltage, go to step 3.

- If there is no voltage, check for:

- - Blown No. B9 fuse - An open or high resistance in the wire

- - Blown No. B9 fuse

Blown No. B9 fuse

- - An open or high resistance in the wire

An open or high resistance in the wire

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Measure the voltage between terminal No. 6 and body ground.

- If there is less than 0.2 V, go to step 4.

- If there is voltage more than 0.2 V, check for:

- - An open or high resistance in the ground wire - Poor ground (G502)

- - An open or high resistance in the ground wire

An open or high resistance in the ground wire

- - Poor ground (G502)

Poor ground (G502)

Measure the voltage between terminal No. 5 and body ground with the shift lever in R position/mode.

- If there is battery voltage, replace the rearview mirror .

- If there is no voltage, check for:

- - An open or high resistance in the wire - Faulty under-dash fuse/relay box - Faulty reverse relay circuit - Faulty transmission range switch

- - An open or high resistance in the wire

An open or high resistance in the wire

- - Faulty under-dash fuse/relay box

Faulty under-dash fuse/relay box

- - Faulty reverse relay circuit

Faulty reverse relay circuit

- - Faulty transmission range switch

Faulty transmission range switch
````

1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. Measure the voltage between terminal No. 7 and body ground.

- If there is battery voltage, go to step 3.

- - An open or high resistance in the ground wire

Poor ground (G502)

## Chunk 3394: Automatic Dimming Mirrors System Description - Components

- Title: Automatic Dimming Mirrors System Description - Components
- Source path: `pages\2466.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_c545b2d19561`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3119.html`, `pages\25436.html`, `pages\18594.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Automatic Dimming Mirrors System Description - Components

Inside Mirror

The front-facing lux level sensor is attached to the front of the mirror housing. Day or night, it senses the amount of ambient light at the front of the vehicle, and that signal is transmitted to the control unit located inside the mirror housing. The rear-facing lux level sensor is attached to the rear of the mirror housing. It senses the amount of light or glare at the rear of the vehicle, and transmits that signal to the control unit.
````

## Chunk 3395: Automatic Dimming Mirrors System Description - Control/Function

- Title: Automatic Dimming Mirrors System Description - Control/Function
- Source path: `pages\2467.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_32cb0a607570`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3120.html`, `pages\25437.html`, `pages\18595.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Automatic Dimming Mirrors System Description - Control/Function

Basic Control/Function

Based on each signal, the control unit judges whether the light at the rear of the vehicle is stronger than the light sensed at the front. If the control unit judges that the light sensed by the rear-facing lux level sensor is brighter than the light sensed by the front-facing lux level sensor, it transmits a signal relaying that information to the drive circuit.

When shifting the transmission to the R position/mode while automatic dimming control is in operation, the automatic dimming turns off and the mirror returns to its highest state of reflectivity. When shifting the transmission out of the R position/mode, the automatic dimming control function is reactivated.
````

## Chunk 3398: Power Mirrors System Description - Components

- Title: Power Mirrors System Description - Components
- Source path: `pages\2470.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_0428b52e9207`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3123.html`, `pages\25440.html`, `pages\18598.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Power Mirrors System Description - Components

Power Mirror

The power mirror has a built-in power mirror actuator and the mirror defogger.

Power Window Master Switch

During power mirror control, the power window master switch operates the mirror defogger on the driver's side via the power mirror switch and the B-CAN signal input.

Passenger's Power Window Switch/Front Passenger's Power Window Switch

During power mirror control, the front passenger's power window switch *2 or the passenger's power window switch *1 receives a signal from the power window master switch via the LIN and operates the mirror defogger on the passenger's side.

*1: 2-door

*2: 4-door
````

## Chunk 3399: Power Mirrors System Description - Control/Function (2/4-door)

- Title: Power Mirrors System Description - Control/Function (2/4-door)
- Source path: `pages\2471.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_03adaa2e41a8`
- Images: `images\GHH410461.jpeg`, `images\GHH410462.jpeg`, `images\GHH410463.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3124.html`, `pages\25441.html`, `pages\18599.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Power Mirrors System Description - Control/Function (2/4-door)

Mirror Defogger Function (With Mirror Defogger)

2-door

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

4-door

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Manual Function

The mirror defogger is controlled by the climate control unit. When the vehicle is in the ON mode and the rear window defogger/mirror defogger switch is pressed, the climate control unit sends the defogger ON signal as a B-CAN signal to the power window master switch. The power window master switch operates the driver's side mirror defogger, and sends the defogger ON signal via the LIN to the front passenger's power window switch to operate the passenger's side mirror defogger.

Auto Mirror Defogger Function

When the power window master switch receives a signal that the outside air temperature is 41 deg.F (5 deg.C) or below from the outside air temperature sensor, the power window master switch automatically turns on the left and right mirror defoggers within 10 seconds after the vehicle is in the ON mode. The mirror defogger output automatically stops in 10 minutes as a result of its timer. If the power window master switch receives a rear window defogger/mirror defogger switch input signal from the climate control unit during power mirror defogger output, the power window master switch shuts off the auto operation function immediately.

Defogger Timer Function

The mirror defogger provides the timer control function that is controlled by the climate control unit. The timer control is operated by turning the vehicle to ON mode, then turning on the rear window defogger/mirror defogger switch. The timer operating time varies according to ambient temperature. If the rear window defogger/mirror defogger switch is turned on when the timer is active, it toggles between ON and OFF every time the switch is pressed.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

# Power Mirrors System Description - Control/Function (5-door) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

Mirror Defogger Function (With Mirror Defogger)

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Manual Function

The mirror defogger is controlled by the climate control unit. When the vehicle is in the ON mode and the rear window defogger/mirror defogger switch is pressed, the climate control unit sends the defogger ON signal as a B-CAN signal to the power window master switch. The power window master switch operates the driver's side mirror defogger, and sends the defogger ON signal via the LIN to the front passenger's power window switch to operate the passenger's side mirror defogger.

Auto Mirror Defogger Function

When the power window master switch receives a signal that the outside air temperature is 41 deg.F (5 deg.C) or below from the outside air temperature sensor, the power window master switch automatically turns on the left and right mirror defoggers within 10 seconds after the vehicle is turned to the ON mode. The mirror defogger output automatically stops in 10 minutes as a result of its timer. If the power window master switch receives a rear window defogger/mirror defogger switch input signal from the climate control unit during power mirror defogger output, the power window master switch shuts off the auto operation function immediately.

Defogger Timer Function

The mirror defogger provides the timer control function that is controlled by the climate control unit. The timer control is operated by turning the vehicle to the ON mode, then turning on the rear window defogger/mirror defogger switch. The timer operating time varies according to ambient temperature. If the rear window defogger/mirror defogger switch is turned on when the timer is active, it toggles between ON and OFF every time the switch is pressed.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

NOTE:

- If you are troubleshooting multiple DTCs, be sure to follow the instructions in B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

B1C55 Driver's Heater Thermistor Circuit Open

# DTC B1C56

DTC B1C56 : Driver's Heater Thermistor Circuit Short

# DTC B1C68

- The front seat heater control unit does not turn on the front seat heater until the seat core temperature is 185°F (85°C) or less. Make sure the seat core temperature is below 185°F (85°C) before troubleshooting.

# DTC U1064 (Front Seat Heater Control Unit)

DTC U1064 : Seat Heater Control Unit Lost Communication with Climate Control Unit

NOTE:

- According to the detected DTC(s), check for the power circuit and the ground circuit of the control unit which cannot communicate with the front seat heater control unit.

- Refer to the DTC shown on the display, then inspect the connectors and terminals based on the instructions.

- If you are troubleshooting multiple DTCs, be sure to follow the instructions in B-CAN System Diagnosis Test Mode A .

- The front seat heater control unit does not turn on the front seat heater until the seat core temperature is 185°F (85°C) or less. Make sure the seat core temperature is below 185°F (85°C) before troubleshooting.

DTC Description | DTC

U1064 Seat Heater Control Unit Lost Communication with Climate Control Unit

DTC (Front Seat Heater Control Unit)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTCs with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode and then the ON mode. -4. Wait for at least 6 seconds. -5. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC U1064 Seat Heater Control Unit Lost Communication with Climate Control Unit Is DTC U1064 indicated? YES Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for loose or poor connections.

-2. Clear the DTCs with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode and then the ON mode.

YES

NO

Disconnect the transmitting control unit connector. Refer to the DTC shown on the display, then disconnect the connector based on the instructions (see table). DTC Connector U1064 Climate control unit connector A (32P) -4. Check for continuity between the receiving control unit and the transmitting control unit on the B-CAN_H circuit and the B-CAN_L circuit. # Seat Heater Replacement (2/4-door): Replacement

SRS components are located in this area. Review the SRS component locations and the precautions and procedures before doing repairs or service.

Front Seat Cushion Heater

- Front Seat Cushion Cover - Remove

- Front Seat Cushion Heater - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. NOTE: The seat cushion heater is glued to the seat cushion pad. When removing it, take care not to damage the seat cushion pad.

NOTE: Reference position in the vertical direction: Align the seat cushion heater edge (A) with the seat cushion pad groove (B). Reference position in the horizontal direction: - Align the seat cushion heater position (C) with the center of the seat cushion pad reference position (D). - Align the center of the seat cushion heater position with the seat cushion pad center line (E). Once the seat heater is in place on the cushion, tuck the bridge folds (F) of the seat heater evenly into the grooves of the seat cushion pad. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

## Chunk 3461: Front Seat Heater Control Unit Connector for Inputs and Outputs

- Title: Front Seat Heater Control Unit Connector for Inputs and Outputs
- Source path: `pages\2525.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_b4022168ecac`
- Images: `images\GHH411277.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3178.html`, `pages\25366.html`, `pages\13095.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Front Seat Heater Control Unit Connector for Inputs and Outputs

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Terminal number | Terminal name | Description | Signal

1 | +B HEATER(DR) | Outputs +B HEATER(DR) signal | With front seat heater operation: about battery voltage If none of above conditions are met: less than 0.2 V

2 | HEATER ECU +B | Power source for front seat heater control unit | With vehicle in ON mode and front seat heater switch ON: about battery voltage

3 | +B HEATER(AS) | Outputs +B HEATER(AS) signal | With front seat heater operation: about battery voltage If none of above conditions are met: less than 0.2 V

4 | GND(HEATER and ECU) *1GND(HEATER and HEATER ECU) *2 | Ground for front seat heater control unit | Less than 0.2 V at all times

5 | Not used | --- | ---

6 | -THERMISTER(AS) *1AS TH- *2 | -THERMISTER(AS) signal *1AS TH- signal *2 | ---

7 | -THERMISTER(DR) *1DR TH- *2 | -THERMISTER(DR) signal *1DR TH- signal *2 | ---

8 | Not used | --- | ---

9 | IG1 | IG1 power source | With vehicle in ON and START mode: about battery voltage

10 | B-CAN_L | Communication line | ---

11 | B-CAN_H | Communication line | ---

12 | +THERMISTER(AS) *1AS TH+ *2 | +THERMISTER(AS) signal *1AS TH+ signal *2 | ---

13 | +THERMISTER(DR) *1DR TH+ *2 | +THERMISTER(DR) signal *1DR TH+ signal *2 | ---

14 | Not used | --- | ---

*1: Without power seat

*2: With power seat
````

# Front Seat Heater Control Unit Input Test

NOTE: Before testing, make sure the No. B5 (10 A), No. B9 (10 A), and No. B32 (20 A) fuses in the under-dash fuse/relay box are OK.

1. Disconnect the front seat heater control unit 14P connector .

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Inspect the connector and socket terminals to be sure they are all making good contact:

- If the terminals are bent, loose or corroded, repair them as necessary, and recheck the system.

- If the terminals look OK, go to step 3.

With the connector still disconnected, do the following input tests:

- If any test indicates a problem, find and correct the cause, then recheck the system.

- If all the input tests prove OK, go to step 4.

Cavity | Wire | Test condition | Test: Desired result | Possible cause if desired result is not obtained

- Faulty driver's seat cushion heater

- An open or high resistance in the wire

12*6 | GRN*BLU | Under all conditions | Check for continuity between terminals No. 12 and No. 6: There should be continuity. | Faulty front passenger's seat cushion heater An open or high resistance in the wire

- Faulty front passenger's seat cushion heater

- An open or high resistance in the wire

Reconnect the connector, and do the following input tests:

- If any test indicates a problem, find and correct the cause, then recheck the system.

- If all the input tests prove OK, replace the front seat heater control unit .

- Faulty front seat heater relay circuit

- An open or high resistance in the wire

9 | RED | Vehicle ON mode | Measure the voltage to ground: There should be battery voltage. | Blown No. B5 (10 A) fuse in the under-dash fuse/relay box An open or high resistance in the wire

- An open or high resistance in the wire

- An open or high resistance in the ground wire

# Seat Heaters System Description - Components

Seat Heater Switches

The front seat heater switches are installed in the climate control panel, and the rear seat heater switches are installed the rear of the center console.

Thermostat and Breaker (Rear Seat)

The seat cushion heater has a thermostat and a breaker. The thermostat and the breaker protect the seat heater circuit from overheat.

Rear Seat Cushion

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

## Chunk 3471: Seat Heaters System Description - Control/Function

- Title: Seat Heaters System Description - Control/Function
- Source path: `pages\2534.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_a1e57e1ca092`
- Images: `images\GHH411302.jpeg`, `images\GHH411303.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3187.html`, `pages\25375.html`, `pages\16172.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Seat Heaters System Description - Control/Function

Heater Control

Front Seat Heaters

The front seat heater control unit sets the seat heater to High/Mid/Low or OFF based on the seat heater switch signals received from the climate control unit via the B-CAN. The front seat heater control unit monitors the thermistor resistance, the voltage/current/temperature to the heater, and the presence of communication with the climate control unit. Also, the system stores DTCs when a malfunction is detected in the front seat heater control unit.

Automatic Operation

Based on the outside air temperature, A/C target temperature and other factors, the climate control unit calculates the optimal front seat heater setting. The climate control unit sends this setting request to the front seat heater control unit via B-CAN. When operating in auto mode or during remote engine start, the front seat heater control unit will operate per the B-CAN setting request from the climate control unit.

Thermistor and Breaker (Front Seats)

The seat cushion heater has a thermistor and breaker. The thermistor and breaker protect the seat heater circuit from overheating. The value of the thermistor is inputted to the control unit.

Except Si

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Si

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The seat heater temperature is controlled by breaker ON and OFF condition.

ON | OFF

Front Seat Cushion Breaker | 113 - 131 deg.F (45 - 55 deg.C) | 167 - 185 deg.F (75 - 85 deg.C)
````

## Chunk 3472: Seat Heaters System Description - Failsafe Function

- Title: Seat Heaters System Description - Failsafe Function
- Source path: `pages\2535.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_3f42e2db9741`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3188.html`, `pages\25376.html`, `pages\16173.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Seat Heaters System Description - Failsafe Function

Thermistor Circuit Open Failure

When the seat heater control unit detects a thermistor wire break, it stops the heater output 90 seconds after detection and turns off the seat heater switch indicator.

Thermistor Circuit Short Failure

When the seat heater control unit detects a thermistor short-circuit, it stops the heater output immediately after detection and turns off the seat heater switch indicator.

Heater Circuit Current Error

When the seat heater control unit detects an abnormal current value in the heater circuit, it stops the heater output immediately after detection and turns off the seat heater switch indicator.
````

## Chunk 3484: Quarter Glass Removal and Installation (2-door) (2016 2017 2018 2019 2020): Removal

- Title: Quarter Glass Removal and Installation (2-door) (2016 2017 2018 2019 2020): Removal
- Source path: `pages\2548.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_8b7613000fb0`
- Images: `images\GHH413085.jpeg`, `images\GHH413086.jpeg`, `images\GHH413087.jpeg`

### Full Text

````text
# Quarter Glass Removal and Installation (2-door) (2016 2017 2018 2019 2020): Removal

- B-Pillar Upper Trim - Remove

- Quarter Pillar Trim - Remove

- Quarter Glass - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Apply the protective tape along the inside and outside edges of the body. Make a hole with an awl through the adhesive from inside the vehicle. Push a piece of piano wire through the hole, and wrap each end around a piece of wood. 2. Remove the quarter glass front seal (A). If necessary, cut the front seal with a utility knife. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Cutting positions Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. With a helper on the outside, pull the piano wire (A) back and forth in a sawing motion. Hold the piano wire as close to the quarter glass (B) as possible to prevent damage to the body, and carefully cut through the adhesive (C) around the entire quarter glass. 4. Carefully remove the quarter glass. 5. Scrape smooth the original adhesive with a knife until there is a thickness of about 1 mm (0.04 in) - 2 mm (0.08 in) on the bonding surface around the entire quarter glass opening flange. NOTE: Do not scrape down to the painted surface of the body; damaged paint will interfere with proper bonding. Remove the clips from the body.

2. Remove the quarter glass front seal (A). If necessary, cut the front seal with a utility knife.

## Chunk 3485: Quarter Glass Removal and Installation (2-door) (2016 2017 2018 2019 2020): Installation

- Title: Quarter Glass Removal and Installation (2-door) (2016 2017 2018 2019 2020): Installation
- Source path: `pages\2549.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_2e384a3153f1`
- Images: `images\GHH413088.jpeg`, `images\GHH413089.jpeg`, `images\GHH413090.jpeg`, `images\GHH413091.jpeg`, `images\GHH413092.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\3242.html`, `pages\25348.html`, `pages\13261.html`

### Full Text

````text
# Quarter Glass Removal and Installation (2-door) (2016 2017 2018 2019 2020): Installation

- Quarter Glass - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Install the quarter glass front seal (A) with the adhesive tape to the molding (B). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. Apply a light coat of the primer to the quarter glass (A) along the edge of the front seal (B) and the molding (C) as shown, then lightly wipe it off with gauze or cheesecloth. NOTE: Apply the primer to the front area (D) and the corner areas (E) of the quarter glass using the printed dots (F) on the quarter glass as a guide. Do not apply the body primer to the quarter glass, and do not mix up the body and glass primer applicators. Never touch the primed surfaces with your hands. If you do, the adhesive may not bond to the quarter glass properly, causing a leak after the quarter glass is installed. Keep water, dust, and abrasive materials away from the primed surfaces. 3. Apply a light coat of the body primer to any exposed paint or metal on the quarter glass mounting flange. Let the body primer dry for at least 10 minutes. NOTE: Do not apply the glass primer to the body. Do not apply the body primer to any remaining original adhesive on the flange. Be careful not to mix up the body and glass primer sponges. Never touch the primed surfaces with your hands. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 4. Cut a "V" in the end of the nozzle (A) on the adhesive cartridge as shown. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 5. Put the cartridge in a caulking gun, and run a continuous bead of adhesive (A) on the quarter glass (B) along the edge of the front seal (C) and the molding (D) as shown. NOTE: Apply the adhesive within 30 minutes after applying the glass primer. Apply the adhesive to the front area (E) and the corner areas (F) using the printed dots (G) on the quarter glass as a guide. Make a slightly thicker bead at each corner. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 6. Hold the quarter glass (A) with the suction cups (B) over the opening, align the clips (C), and set it down on the adhesive. Lightly push on the quarter glass until its edges are fully seated on the adhesive all the way around. NOTE: Do not open or close any of the doors for about an hour until the adhesive is dry. 7. Remove the excess adhesive with a putty knife or a shop towel dampened with isopropyl alcohol. 8. Wait at least an hour for the adhesive to dry, then spray water over the quarter glass and check for leaks. Mark the leaking areas, and let the quarter glass dry, then seal with sealant. Let the vehicle stand for at least 4 hours after quarter glass installation. If the vehicle has to be used within the first 4 hours, it must be driven slowly. 9. Advise the customer not to do the following things for 2 to 3 days: Slam the doors with all the windows rolled up. Twist the body excessively (such as when going in and out of driveways at an angle or driving over rough, uneven roads).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 1. Install the quarter glass front seal (A) with the adhesive tape to the molding (B).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 2. Apply a light coat of the primer to the quarter glass (A) along the edge of the front seal (B) and the molding (C) as shown, then lightly wipe it off with gauze or cheesecloth. NOTE: Apply the primer to the front area (D) and the corner areas (E) of the quarter glass using the printed dots (F) on the quarter glass as a guide. Do not apply the body primer to the quarter glass, and do not mix up the body and glass primer applicators. Never touch the primed surfaces with your hands. If you do, the adhesive may not bond to the quarter glass properly, causing a leak after the quarter glass is installed. Keep water, dust, and abrasive materials away from the primed surfaces. 3. Apply a light coat of the body primer to any exposed paint or metal on the quarter glass mounting flange. Let the body primer dry for at least 10 minutes. NOTE: Do not apply the glass primer to the body. Do not apply the body primer to any remaining original adhesive on the flange. Be careful not to mix up the body and glass primer sponges. Never touch the primed surfaces with your hands.

- Apply the primer to the front area (D) and the corner areas (E) of the quarter glass using the printed dots (F) on the quarter glass as a guide.

- Do not apply the body primer to the quarter glass, and do not mix up the body and glass primer applicators.

- Never touch the primed surfaces with your hands.
````

# Quarter Glass Removal and Installation (4-door): Installation

- Quarter Glass - Install Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Apply a light coat of the primer to the quarter glass (A) along the edge of the front seal (B) and the molding (C) as shown, then lightly wipe it off with gauze or cheesecloth. NOTE: Apply the primer to the corner areas (D) of the quarter glass using the printed dots (E) on the quarter glass as a guide. Do not apply the body primer to the quarter glass, and do not mix up the body and glass primer applicators. Never touch the primed surfaces with your hands. If you do, the adhesive may not bond to the quarter glass properly, causing a leak after the quarter glass is installed. Keep water, dust, and abrasive materials away from the primed surfaces. 2. Apply a light coat of the body primer to any exposed paint or metal on the quarter glass mounting flange. Let the body primer dry for at least 10 minutes. NOTE: Do not apply the glass primer to the body. Do not apply the body primer to any remaining original adhesive on the flange. Be careful not to mix up the body and glass primer sponges. Never touch the primed surfaces with your hands. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. Cut a "V" in the end of the nozzle (A) on the adhesive cartridge as shown. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 4. Put the cartridge in a caulking gun, and run a continuous bead of adhesive (A) on the quarter glass (B) along the edge of the front seal (C) and the molding (D) as shown. NOTE: Apply the adhesive within 30 minutes after applying the glass primer. Apply the adhesive to the corner areas (E) using the printed dots (F) on the quarter glass as a guide. Make a slightly thicker bead at each corner. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 5. Hold the quarter glass (A) with the suction cups (B) over the opening, align the clips (C), and set it down on the adhesive. Lightly push on the quarter glass until its edges are fully seated on the adhesive all the way around. NOTE: Do not open or close any of the doors for about an hour until the adhesive is dry. 6. Remove the excess adhesive with a putty knife or a shop towel dampened with isopropyl alcohol. 7. Wait at least an hour for the adhesive to dry, then spray water over the quarter glass and check for leaks. Mark the leaking areas, and let the quarter glass dry, then seal with sealant. ````text
# Rear Window Removal and Installation (2-door) (2016 2017 2018 2019 2020): Removal

- Rear Shelf - Remove

- High Mount Brake Light Cover - Remove

- Rear Window - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Disconnect the terminals (A). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. Pull down the rear area of the headliner (A). NOTE: Take care not to bend the headliner excessively, or you may crease or break it. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. If the original rear window will be reinstalled, make the alignment marks across the rear window and the body with a grease pencil. 4. Apply the protective tape along the inside and outside edges of the body. Make a hole with an awl through the adhesive from inside the vehicle at a corner of the rear window. Push a piece of piano wire through the hole, and wrap each end around a piece of wood. 5. Open the trunk lid. 6. Remove the rubber dam (A) from the lower edge of the rear window (B). If necessary, cut the rubber dam with a utility knife. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Cutting Positions Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 7. With an assistant on the outside, pull the piano wire (A) back and forth in a sawing motion. Hold the piano wire as close to the rear window (B) as possible to prevent damage to the body, and carefully cut through the adhesive (C) around the entire rear window. 8. Carefully remove the rear window. 9. Scrape smooth the original adhesive with a knife, until there is a thickness of about 1 mm (0.04 in) - 2 mm (0.08 in) on the bonding surface around the entire rear window opening flange. NOTE: Do not scrape down to the painted surface of the body; damaged paint will interfere with proper bonding. Remove the clips and the fasteners from the body. 10. Clean the body bonding surface with a shop towel dampened with isopropyl alcohol. After cleaning, keep oil, grease, and water from getting on the surface. 11. If you are reinstalling the original rear window, scrape off the original adhesive, the clips, the fasteners, and the rubber dams from the rear window with a putty knife. Clean the bonding surfaces on the inside face and the edge of the rear window with isopropyl alcohol. Make sure the bonding surface is kept free of water, oil, and grease.

11. Wait at least an hour for the adhesive to dry, then spray water over the rear window and check for leaks. Mark the leaking areas, let the rear window dry, then seal with sealant. Let the vehicle stand for at least 4 hours after rear window installation. If the vehicle has to be used within the first 4 hours, it must be driven slowly.

12. Advise the customer not to do the following things for 2 to 3 days:

- Do not slam the doors with all the windows rolled up.

- Do not twist the body excessively (such as when going in and out of driveways at an angle or driving over rough, uneven roads).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 13. Install the rear area of the headliner (A).

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. | 14. Connect the terminals (A).

- High Mount Brake Light Cover - Install

- Rear Shelf - Install
````

# Rear Window Removal and Installation (4/5-door): Removal

4-door

- Rear Shelf - Remove

- Rear Window - Remove Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 1. Disconnect the terminals (A). Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 2. Pull down the rear area of the headliner (A). NOTE: Take care not to bend the headliner excessively, or you may crease or break it. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 3. If the original rear window will be reinstalled, make the alignment marks across the rear window and the body with a grease pencil. 4. Apply the protective tape along the inside and outside edges of the body. Make a hole with an awl through the adhesive from inside the vehicle at a corner of the rear window. Push a piece of piano wire through the hole, and wrap each end around a piece of wood. 5. Open the trunk lid. 6. Remove the rubber dam (A) from the lower edge of the rear window (B). If necessary, cut the rubber dam with a utility knife. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Cutting Positions Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.HONDA, U.S.A., INC. 7. With an assistant on the outside, pull the piano wire (A) back and forth in a sawing motion. Hold the piano wire as close to the rear window (B) as possible to prevent damage to the body, and carefully cut through the adhesive (C) around the entire rear window. 8. Carefully remove the rear window. 9. Scrape smooth the original adhesive with a knife, until there is a thickness of about 1 mm (0.04 in) - 2 mm (0.08 in) on the bonding surface around the entire rear window opening flange. NOTE: Do not scrape down to the painted surface of the body; damaged paint will interfere with proper bonding. Remove the clips and the fasteners from the body. 10. Clean the body bonding surface with a shop towel dampened with isopropyl alcohol. After cleaning, keep oil, grease, and water from getting on the surface. 11. If you are reinstalling the original rear window, scrape off the original adhesive, the clips, the fasteners, and the rubber dams from the rear window with a putty knife. Clean the bonding surfaces on the inside face and the edge of the rear window with isopropyl alcohol. Make sure the bonding surface is kept free of water, oil, and grease.
